The identity proves the connection between those specified objects; it does not select new ones.

14.6A common short-range covariance on every period

This section records the finite-period covariance output used by the local reader comparison. The construction keeps one bed, one forest, one blocking history, and one cover row throughout. In particular, the row is not chosen again when the period changes. The exact curvature covariance on the tree slice is identified in Subsection 14.5; we use that identity here and construct its common exponentially localized cover representative and its finite-range approximation.

14.6.1Period folding and the common inverse

Let \(\mathcal{I}\) be the finite set of asked coordinates for a fixed forest and gauge branch. Its labels include the intra-block residue and colour. Let \(\mathcal{C}\) denote the curvature labels, likewise including the intra-block cochain type and colour. The lattice coordinate in a cover row is always a coarse cell base, not a fine site. Write \(d_n\) for shortest \(\ell^1\) distance between coarse bases on the period-\(n\) torus. A cover row is a family \(P_{ab} (z)\), \(a, b \in \mathcal{I}\), \(z \in \mathbb{Z}^4\). Its period-\(n\) matrix is the absolutely convergent periodization

\(\displaystyle P^{(n)}_{(x, a), (y, b)} = \sum_{z : y = x + z \hspace{0.27em} \text{mod} \hspace{0.27em} n} P_{ab} (z) .\)

A bound \(\sum_z e^{s |z|_1} |P_{ab} (z) | \leqslant M\) makes this sum absolute and bounds each row uniformly in the period. All sums below use the same coordinate and colour labels.

Lemma 14.3. (Folding a coercive row to every period) Suppose \(P\) is a real symmetric cover row, has an exponential row bound of width \(s > 0\), and there are \(a > 0\) and \(n_0\) such that

\(\displaystyle \langle v, P^{(n)} v \rangle \geqslant a \|v\|_2^2 \quad \text{for every } n \geqslant n_0 .\)

If the periodizations commute with pullback under every quotient map \(\mathbb{T}_m^4 \to \mathbb{T}_n^4\) with \(n \mid m\), then the same lower bound holds for every positive period.

Proof. Fix \(n\). Choose \(m = (n_0 + 1) n\) and let \(g\) be coordinatewise reduction modulo \(n\). Then \(g\) is surjective and commutes with lattice translations. Pull a vector \(v\) on period \(n\) back to \(v \circ g\). Each coordinate of \(v\) has exactly \(| \ker g|\) preimages, so

\(\displaystyle \|v \circ g\|_2^2 = | \ker g| \|v\|_2^2 .\)

At a coordinate \((x, i)\), the left side is

\(\displaystyle \sum_{j \in \mathcal{I}} \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^4} P_{ij} (z) \hspace{0.17em} v (g (x + z), j) .\)

Since \(g (x + z) = g (x) + g (z)\), group the absolutely convergent sum by the residue \(g (z)\). The result is \((P^{(n)} v) (g (x), i)\), so \(P^{(m)} (v \circ g) = (P^{(n)} v) \circ g\). Taking inner products and using the same fiber count on both sides gives

\(\displaystyle | \ker g| \langle v, P^{(n)} v \rangle \geqslant a | \ker g| \|v\|_2^2 .\)

The fiber size is positive, so division proves the claim. This argument also applies with the finite colour coordinate present: the quotient acts only on the spatial coordinate.\(\Box\)

The inverse row is obtained without a Fourier-symbol assumption. For each period, take the ordinary matrix inverse of \(P^{(n)}\). A uniform positive lower bound gives the finite-volume resolvent bound. The exponential row estimate gives a uniform weighted row and column bound on every periodization: the internal label set is finite and each periodic displacement sum is bounded by the corresponding cover sum. The diagonal conjugation estimate of Subsection 8.13, applied to these finite matrices, gives constants \(C_1, r_1 > 0\), independent of the period, such that each inverse entry is bounded by \(C_1 e^{- r_1 d_n (x, y)}\). This is the finite-dimensional weighted-resolvent estimate used in the period-independent inverse construction; no spectral lower bound at complex momentum is assumed. For every factorial side \(N = k\)!, choose one centered shortest representative in each residue class, coordinatewise in \([- \lfloor N / 2 \rfloor, \lceil N / 2 \rceil)\). Define the cover coefficient read from its inverse at \((0, a)\) and \((z, b)\) when \(z\) is this representative, and set it to zero otherwise. For every retained coefficient, \(d_N (0, z) = |z|_1\), so the bound \(C_1 e^{- r_1 |z|_1}\) holds uniformly in both \(z\) and \(k\). For every fixed \(z\), it is retained for all sufficiently large \(k\). Enumerating the countable set of triples and taking successive subsequences, followed by the diagonal subsequence, gives simultaneous coefficient convergence. Passing to finite partial sums retains the exponential bound. At any fixed smaller width, the remaining tails are dominated by a constant times \(e^{- c |z|_1}\), a summable function on \(\mathbb{Z}^4\); hence the inverse rows converge in the weighted row norm.

The limit has the required inverse on every period, not merely on the factorial sequence. Indeed, for \(n \mid m\), pullback intertwines the two periodic convolution matrices. Since both are invertible, it also intertwines their inverses: apply the forward intertwining identity and uniqueness of the solution to the finite linear system. Periodizing the cover limit along any fixed period is justified by the common exponential majorant. Passing to the limit in the factorial periods divisible by \(n\) therefore identifies that periodization with \((P^{(n)})^{- 1}\). This is the period-independent inverse construction used below.

14.6.2The selected tree slice on the same history

Fix a blocking patch, gauge, kernel, forest, and bed \(H\). At each period \(n\), repeat the same forest by the zero-offset lift. Let \(\mathcal{A}_n\) be the fine one-form coordinates and let \(\mathcal{I}_n\) be the active asked-coordinate set. The cover coefficients \(P_{ij} (z)\) are the common full, uncut row on the finite asked-label set \(\mathcal{I}\). Periodization sums these coefficients over the displacement classes and gives exactly the restriction of the finite tree precision to the repeated forest.

The local construction has this concrete input. Fix one odd block side \(L\), one patch and gauge branch, and one forest on the reference three-side block. Repeat that forest at every period by the same zero-offset rule. Fix a bed \(H\), a clip, and constants \(s, M, a, V, K_{\text{pen}}\) with \(s, a, K_{\text{pen}} > 0\), \(M \geqslant 0\), and \(V > 0\). Let \(G_n\) and \(\Delta_n\) be its metric and diagonal matrices on period \(n\). Assume \(H\) has strip width \(s\) and bound \(M\), and that at each finite period these matrices are symmetric and satisfy

\(\displaystyle a \|u\|_2^2 \leqslant \langle u, G_n u \rangle \leqslant V \|u\|_2^2, \qquad a \|u\|_2^2 \leqslant \langle u, \Delta_n u \rangle \leqslant V \|u\|_2^2 .\)

Also assume the exact root-gradient identity (14.28), the same-bed stationarity identities (14.33), and the two-form transport identity (14.34) from Subsection 14.5. The displayed numerical bounds produce the gap; these algebraic identities identify the conditional covariance. They are distinct inputs. The construction fixes its positive width \(t\) and row constant \(A\) from the patch, kernel, branch, forest template, and \(s, M, a\) before selecting the bed within this class; the same pair works uniformly for every bed satisfying the stated strip and floor bounds.

The finite block estimate of Subsection 11.21 gives the lower bound

\(\displaystyle \frac{\min (a, K_{\text{pen}})}{1 + \beta + \gamma}, \qquad \beta, \gamma \geqslant 0,\)

for every coarse side at least \(100 R + 3\), where \(R\) is the macro radius and \(K_{\text{pen}}\) is the prescribed penalty. Here \(X\) is the fixed patch-offset set and \(C_{\text{edge}}\) is the edge-comparison constant. The finite costs are

\(\displaystyle \beta_{\ell} = (2 C_{\text{edge}}^2) | \text{Site} (L) | \hspace{0.17em} | \text{Axis} | (|X| \hspace{0.17em} | \text{PlaqAxes} |) \hspace{0.17em} |X|, \qquad \gamma_{\ell} = 2 | \text{Site} (L) | .\)

They are the curl and rooted-probe coefficients proved in Subsection 11.21; in the gap formula above \(\beta = \beta_{\ell}\) and \(\gamma = \gamma_{\ell}\). The cover coefficients are obtained by composing the bed row with the fixed local blocking maps. The weighted row norm of a product is bounded by the product of the weighted row norms, since \(|z + w|_1 \leqslant |z|_1 + |w|_1\). The blocking maps have finite coefficient sums, so the resulting exponential bound is finite and independent of the period. Periodizing these same coefficients gives the repeated full row, whose restriction is the tree precision. Thus it has the displayed gap at all sufficiently large periods. For any requested period \(n\), take \(m = (100 R + 4) n\), reduce modulo \(n\), and pull a vector back. The quotient is surjective and every fiber has the same positive cardinality. Directly reindexing the cover convolution gives \(P^{(m)} (v \circ g) = (P^{(n)} v) \circ g\), while both the quadratic form and squared norm of \(v \circ g\) are multiplied by \(| \ker g|\). Dividing transfers the same gap to period \(n\).

Apply the weighted finite-resolvent estimate to this common row. Its inverse is one exponentially localized cover row whose period action is the inverse tree precision at every period. Let \(\mathcal{R}\) be the fixed rooted path-measurement matrix from asked-coordinate vectors to curvature coordinates, so its rows are indexed by \(\mathcal{C}\) and columns by \(\mathcal{I}\). The rooted gauge projection and the identity between the root correction and the gradient evaluation show that the curvature is unchanged by removing forest coordinates. Thus the matrix

\(\displaystyle Z =\mathcal{R} \hspace{0.17em} P^{- 1}_{\text{row}} \hspace{0.17em} \mathcal{R}^{\textsf{T}}\)

has, on each period, the same restriction to active curvature coordinates as the finite tree-slice covariance for every admissible coarse period \(n \geqslant 3\). More explicitly, the matrix on the slice is \((DI_F) A_F^{- 1} (DI_F)^{\textsf{T}}\), which equals \(P_f - JP_c J^{\textsf{T}}\) by (14.37). This is an exact equality. Its proof uses the same bed and the same forest on both sides; it does not replace a curved bed by the flat one or compare two different histories. The finite-dimensional curl-through identity and the resulting exact conditional curvature formula are the ones proved in Subsection 14.5.

The row norm of \(Z\) is bounded by the product of the row norms of the two rooted path rows and the inverse row. For convolution, the weighted row norm is submultiplicative: expand the product, use \(|z + w|_1 \leqslant |z|_1 + |w|_1\), and sum the resulting nonnegative majorant. Therefore \(Z\) has a positive exponential width, with a finite constant fixed before the period is chosen.

If the desired period matrices are symmetric but this particular cover representative is not symmetric term by term, replace it by

\(\displaystyle Z^{\text{sym}}_{ab} (z) = \tfrac{1}{2} (Z_{ab} (z) + Z_{ba} (- z)) .\)

Its period matrix is the average of the original matrix and its transpose. Since the exact tree covariance is symmetric, this replacement leaves every period matrix unchanged. The triangle inequality shows that its exponential row bound is no larger than the original bound. The reversal is essential: transposition reverses the displacement as well as exchanging the two indices.

14.6.3Cutting to short paths

For a scale parameter \(x > 1\), set the integer radius \(B_x = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil\) and the natural-number cut radii \(h_x = \lfloor B_x / 10000 \rfloor\) and \(H_x = \lfloor B_x / 8 \rfloor\). Their real uses below mean their natural casts. Choose a symmetric coarse-cell displacement cut which retains every coefficient with \(|z|_1 \leqslant h_x\) and retains none with \(|z|_1 > H_x\). Write \(Z^{\leqslant x}\) for the cut row. If \(\sum_z e^{t |z|_1} |Z_{ab} (z) | \leqslant A\), then for every finite-period entry the triangle inequality and the tail estimate give

\(\displaystyle | Z^{(n)}_{(u, a), (v, b)} - (Z^{\leqslant x})^{(n)}_{(u, a), (v, b)} | \leqslant Ae^{- th_x} .\)

The same estimate holds with row and column interchanged. The cut matrix is symmetric because the retention rule is invariant under \((a, b, z) \mapsto (b, a, - z)\). Also, if the separation of the two spatial coarse parent-base addresses (the addresses obtained by splitting the fine site into its coarse base and intra-block residue) is greater than \(H_x\), its cut period-matrix entry is zero: every retained coarse displacement has length at most \(H_x\), so none can connect those bases modulo the period. These two facts are the only geometric properties of the cut used in the short-history comparison.

For an input site and label, sum the omitted absolute mass over all output sites and labels. For each output label, the triangle inequality in the periodization sum bounds the omitted mass by the cover sum above. Summing over the output-label set \(\mathcal{C}\) thus gives the row bound

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}} (t, A, x) = |\mathcal{C}| A \exp (- th_x) .\)

For the installed two-form path row, the cover operator is

\(\displaystyle \Psi = D_f^{- 1} \hspace{0.17em} \text{Fine} (T) \hspace{0.17em} D_c,\)

where \(D_f\) is the fine diagonal, \(D_c\) the coarse diagonal, and \(\text{Fine} (T)\) is the installed tower map. Assume the corresponding same-history strip estimates: \(D_f^{- 1}\) has bound \(M_f\), \(D_c\) has bound \(M_c\), and the moving tower row has bound \(M_T / L^2\). The weighted row-product estimate gives

\(\displaystyle \Psi \text{ has strip bound } \frac{m_2^2 M_f M_T M_c}{L^2} .\)

In the selected case where the three incoming factors are all bounded by \(M_{\text{in}}\), put \(\psi_{\text{budget}} (u) = m_2^2 u^3\); then the installed row has bound \(\psi_{\text{budget}} (M_{\text{in}}) / L^2\). The factor \(L^{- 2}\) is already in the moving tower receipt, not produced by installation; installing the operator in residue coordinates preserves the bound on each cover sum. Let \(\mathcal{C}_2\) be the set of two-form labels. The movement error is bounded by \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}_2} (s, \psi_{\text{budget}} (M_{\text{in}}) / L^2, x)\). The turn-row error uses its own output labels and strip parameters.

Define the short transport matrix on a period by keeping each installed transport entry when the two coarse parent bases have separation at most \(H_x\), and setting it to zero otherwise. The cut path row has zero at all omitted addresses. The installed-row estimate therefore bounds, for each input coordinate, the omitted row sum by \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}_2} (s, \psi_{\text{budget}} (M_{\text{in}}) / L^2, x)\); in particular every omitted entry has this bound, while retained entries are unchanged. This is the same two-case finite-range comparison as for \(Q_n^{\text{short}}\) below.

Proposition 14.4. (Cutting the constructed same-history covariance) Under the fixed branch, repeated-forest, bed strip/floor, and same-bed factorization hypotheses above, construct \(Z\) by the common inverse and rooted measurement. If a symmetric cut retains every displacement through \(h_x\), has support through \(H_x\), and its row-tail payment is at most \(\varepsilon\) in both orientations, then \(Z\) has the exact finite tree-slice curvature covariance at every admissible coarse period \(n \geqslant 3\), while the cut differs entrywise from that covariance by at most \(\varepsilon\). Its entries vanish at separations greater than \(H_x\).

Proof. The finite tree bound gives the gap above the stated side threshold, and Lemma 14.3 transfers it to every period. The period-independent inverse and rooted measurement produce \(Z\) with the exact period matrices for the admissible coarse periods \(n \geqslant 3\). Symmetrize by displacement reversal; exact symmetry of the period covariances preserves every period output. The cut-tail estimate gives the entrywise error, and the support-radius condition gives the vanishing statement.\(\Box\)

There is a useful finite-range version of the cut output. On period \(n\), let \(C_n = Z^{(n)}\) be the exact covariance and define

\(\displaystyle Q^{\text{short}}_n (u, a ; v, b) = \left\{ \begin{array}{ll} (C_n)_{(u, a), (v, b)}, & d_n (u, v) \leqslant H_x,\\ 0, & d_n (u, v) > H_x . \end{array} \right.\)

This is symmetric and has range \(H_x\). If \(d_n (u, v) \leqslant H_x\), it agrees with the covariance by definition. If \(d_n (u, v) > H_x\), the cut period matrix vanishes by the support condition, so the bound from Proposition 14.4 gives

\(\displaystyle | (C_n)_{(u, a), (v, b)} - Q^{\text{short}}_n (u, a ; v, b) | \leqslant \mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}} (t, A, x) .\)

Thus the short comparison used at a reader is exact on retained pairs; the only error is the omitted covariance tail. This is the two-case form needed for a finite-range covariance comparator on one fixed history.

14.6.4How the estimate enters the path telescope

For clarity, the quantitative input used by the path telescope consists of two payments, not a free approximation assertion. If \(\Psi\) is the installed two-form path row, let \(m_2\) be the number of two-form labels, set \(\psi_{\text{budget}} (u) = m_2^2 u^3\), and let \(M_{\text{in}}\) be its pre-installation bound. The movement payment is

\(\displaystyle m_2 \hspace{0.17em} \frac{\psi_{\text{budget}} (M_{\text{in}})}{L^2} \exp (- sh_x) \leqslant \varepsilon .\)

The second payment asks for a same-bed row \(Z\), a width \(t > 0\), and a bound \(A > 0\) such that the preceding cut estimate is at most \(\varepsilon\). The path part of the telescope uses the movement receipt through the triangle inequality and the finite row sum of the installed path data; the covariance part uses the turn receipt and the exact same-bed transfer. These are separate clauses. This section proves the row and covariance estimate, but it does not derive either bound from the scale schedule.

Suppose two consecutive levels of the selected history have the same blocking clock. In the covariance clause, truncate the rooted row at radius \(H_x\). For endpoints farther apart, the truncated matrix entry vanishes. For the remaining pairs, the exact same-bed transfer identifies the uncut entry with the finite tree conditional covariance, and the cut estimate bounds its replacement error. In the path clause, the installed path row is handled by its finite row-sum estimate and the separate movement receipt. Both clauses retain the original stamped history. The selected schedule must allocate the movement and turn bounds to their respective requested tolerances; the field-path bound and capital budget remain additional hypotheses of the telescope.

The large-period gap, the strip width, and both payments are properties of the selected bed and the selected history. Their production is not proved by the finite covariance algebra above. In particular, this result must be combined with the separate schedule and paid-budget construction before it can be used as an unconditional Yang–Mills construction step.

14.7Gaussian comparison in the small-field window

Fix the outer scale \(x\) and let \(B\) denote its outer radius. The sector panel radius is

\(\displaystyle p = \left\lfloor \frac{B + 80}{20} \right\rfloor .\)

The matrices, forest, frozen coordinates, masks, and cutoff below are always those of the outer-\(B\) construction. The smaller \(p\) is the panel already chosen by that construction. Write \(\mathcal{D}_U (R)\) for the seed-dome of radius \(R\) in the fixed configuration \(U\). Outer clearance means \(o \not\in \mathcal{D}_U (11 B + 1)\).

Lemma 14.5. (Two-scale containment) If \(B \geqslant 200\), then

\(\displaystyle \ \begin{array}{rlll} p & \leqslant B / 20 + 4, & 30 p & < 11 B + 1 - p,\\ 9 p & \leqslant B, & B / 4 + p + B / 50 & \leqslant B / 2,\\ B / 4 + 2 p + B / 25 & \leqslant B, & p + B / 50 + B / 4 + B / 2 & \leqslant B. \end{array}\) (14.38)

Thus a radius-\(8 p\) ball centered at any panel center within distance \(p\) of \(o\) lies in the outer radius-\(B\) ball. A target within \(B / 4\) and its row span of at most \(B / 50\) lie inside the radius-\(B / 2\) region where the source-row bound is available. Adding the further dependency radius \(B / 2\) still stays inside the outer \(B\) neighborhood. Every panel center moved by at most \(p\) remains farther than \(30 p\) from each seed.

Proof. Taking the floor gives \(p \leqslant (B + 80) / 20 = B / 20 + 4\). The seed-dome radius is \(10 B + B + 1 = 11 B + 1\). Moving a center by at most \(p\) leaves clearance at least \(11 B + 1 - p\), and

\(\displaystyle 11 B + 1 - p - 30 p \geqslant 11 B + 1 - 31 (B + 80) / 20 = (189 B - 2460) / 20 > 0.\)

Also \(9 p \leqslant 9 (B + 80) / 20 \leqslant B\) for \(B \geqslant 200\). The total-reach bound follows from

\(\displaystyle p + B / 50 + B / 4 + B / 2 \leqslant B / 20 + 4 + B / 50 + B / 4 + B / 2 = (41 B + 200) / 50 \leqslant B.\)

Finally, \(B / 4 + p + B / 50 \leqslant B / 2\) for \(B \geqslant 200\). These inequalities and \(B / 4 + 2 p + B / 25 \leqslant B\) follow on substituting \(p \leqslant B / 20 + 4\). They concern only support and source queries; no matrix, cutoff or mask is changed.\(\Box\)

Proposition 14.6. (Actual local mean and panel covariance) Fix \(R \geqslant 1\) and put

\(\displaystyle \chi (x) = 16 \{2 (B + 1) + 1\}^5 n_{\text{plaq}} \hspace{0.17em} \vartheta (x) .\)

Here \(n_{\text{plaq}}\) is the finite number of plaquette orientations, \(\vartheta (x)\) is the temperature of the outer construction, and \(B = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil\). There are a fixed finite number of row, color, and local jet types, and a radius-\(r\) ball in the four-dimensional lattice has at most \((2 r + 1)^4\) sites. Consequently the row and panel counts below are bounded by a fixed multiple of \((1 + B)^4\). Fix one patch, tube, forest, tag, posed configuration and history over the entire panel. Assume the selected predictor branch and funded tube, with the fine and coarse beds attached to their respective path rows. The two beds satisfy the positive floor and signed symmetry conditions. On these same beds and tower assume the diagonal and transport identities (8.109), (14.33) and (14.34). Separately, fix the full flat stack for these two beds, their selected clips and this tower. It satisfies the isometry, predictor normalization and adjoint orthogonality in (8.60). Write \(W_c = B_c d_c\) and \(W_f = B_f d_f\) for the stacked curvature maps including the discrete curl, as in Subsection 8.14, \(A_0\) for the installed one-form transport, and \(K_c = W_c^{\ast} W_c\) for the coarse quadratic form. For the penalty \(\kappa > 0\) of the selected predictor and the fine, predictor and remaining blocks \(F_0, I_0, N_0\) of this stack, assume its output identities

\(\displaystyle F_0 W_c = W_f A_0, \qquad I_0 W_c = \kappa^{- 1 / 2} K_c, \qquad N_0 W_c = 0.\)

Here \(\kappa\) is the coefficient denoted \(p\) in (8.60). The rooted adjoint pairing (8.61) and the row cancellation (8.111) use this same response. Use the branch and side conditions of Subsection 14.5, assume \(c \geqslant 100 (1 + B)\), and match the stored flags to the posed configuration at temperature \(\vartheta (x)\). Let \(\delta > 0\) be the fixed small-field threshold. For each \(x\) under consideration, fix \(t\) with \(0 \leqslant t \leqslant \min \{\delta, R \chi (x)\}\). Assume the direction \(v\) has size at most \(t\), its group representative agrees with the posed field on the outer ball, and the local chart bounds for the replica hold on the same ball with bound \(t\). The center lies outside \(\mathcal{D}_U (11 B + 1)\). Use the actual panels selected by the outer construction and the official-address map to the cleared panel. The mean conclusion is for an active coordinate \(i\) whose anchor is within \(B / 4\) of \(o\); the column conclusion is for every actual active row \(j\) of the padded window inverses. Outside the selected panel both padded inverses vanish, so this also includes those rows.

Let \(\eta > 0\) be the minimum decay rate in the short-kernel approximation and long-path budget. Put

\(\displaystyle \text{widen}_x (t) = (1 + \text{pathCutoff} (x)) t + e^{- \eta \text{pathCutoff} (x)} .\) (14.39)

Since \(t \leqslant R \chi (x)\) and \(\chi (x) \to 0\), \(t\) lies below any fixed positive chart threshold for all sufficiently large \(x\). Let \(D_x\) be the number of source rows that can contribute to a fixed normal-residual coordinate; the four-dimensional row count gives \(D_x \leqslant C (1 + B)^4\). Let \(n_p\) bound the number of coordinates in a reader panel at radius \(p\). There are finitely many color and local jet types, so the four-dimensional panel count gives \(n_p \leqslant C (1 + p)^4 \leqslant C (1 + B)^4\). Assume the fixed-row chart and path bounds, the positive Gram gaps \(\lambda, \Lambda > 0\) for the actual and bare matrices with their uniform row constants, and the inverse-window bounds of Subsection 8.13 on these same matrices. Then there is one large-\(x\) threshold depending only on the fixed gauge, patch and branch parameters and the common chart and path constants. It is uniform over the admissible tubes, stores, centres, posed configurations, directions, forests, tags, periods and \(t\) satisfying the stated bounds. Within each comparison all readings still use the same data. Above this threshold,

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} | \mu_i - a_i | & \leqslant x^{- 7 / 8}, \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.40)} \\ | ((Q_V |_{W_i})^{- 1})_{ji} - ((Q_1 |_{W_i^0})^{- 1})_{\bar{j} \bar{i}} | & \leqslant x^{- 1 / 4} . \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.41)} \end{array}\)

Here \(a_i\) is the frozen stationary-response coordinate, \(\mu_i\) is the actual local Gaussian mean, \(Q_V\) and \(Q_1\) are respectively the actual and identity-configuration row Gram matrices, \(W_i\) is the actual reader panel, \(W_i^0\) is its cleared counterpart, and bars denote the official-address bijection. The matrices are not asserted to have equal entries. The error in the second line is the local actual-to-cleared inverse-column comparison; the later covariance split that upgrades it to the full physical-tensor estimate is not part of this proposition.

Proof. The nonlinear row error. Set \(m\) to be the frozen stationary response to \(v\). The exact first-order identity for each uncut row is

\(\displaystyle I_r (m) + F_r (v) = 0,\) (14.42)

where \(F_r (v)\) is the linear first variation and \(I_r (m)\) is the corresponding flat pulse. Write \(B_r (V)\) for the smooth base contribution and \(P_r (V, m)\) for the marked nonlinear pulse. The selected row, after subtraction of its stationary linear part, is exactly

\(\displaystyle \Delta_r = s [(B_r (V) - F_r (v)) + (P_r (V, m) - I_r (m))] .\)

To verify this row formula, restrict to the finite read stencil. Outer clearance makes every free bond there active, while the frozen response \(m\) vanishes on tree bonds. Thus the marked direction agrees with \(m\) throughout the stencil. The additional frozen-input field also vanishes there, so finite row dependence removes it from the selected row formula. No value outside the stencil is used. Indeed, the selected row is \(s (B_r (V) + P_r (V, m))\), while (14.42) says \(F_r (v) + I_r (m) = 0\). Unselected rows vanish by the row mask. This cancellation is made before taking absolute values.

For the base difference, write \(F_j\) for the actual transport factor at the \(j\)th position of a path and \(H_j\) for its frozen linear factor. Expand their finite transport products by the exact telescoping identity

\(\displaystyle F_1 \cdots F_m - H_1 \cdots H_m = \sum_{j = 1}^m F_1 \cdots F_{j - 1} (F_j - H_j) H_{j + 1} \cdots H_m .\)

Write \(w_x = \text{widen}_x (t)\). The chart bounds give a linear curvature row \(v'\) of size at most \(Ct\) and a nonlinear row \(v_0\) with \(|v_0 - v' | \leqslant Ctw_x\). The actual and flat transport kernels \(K, H\) have row norms at most \(C\) and difference row norm at most \(Cw_x\). Thus

\(\displaystyle Kv_0 - Hv' = (K - H) v' + K (v_0 - v'), \qquad |Kv_0 - Hv' | \leqslant 2 C^2 tw_x .\)

The extra factor \(t\) comes from the linear curvature row, not from the transport telescoping alone. The local bond-response Taylor remainder is at most \(Ct^2 \leqslant Ctw_x\). Combining these estimates with the fine curvature-row remainder gives \(|B_r (V) - F_r (v) | \leqslant At \hspace{0.17em} \text{widen}_x (t)\). For the pulse difference, first restrict the direction to the outer comparison ball of radius \(B\). Finite row dependence makes the row insensitive to the discarded part. The pulse construction then cuts paths at \(\text{pathCutoff} (x)\): its finite pulse bound is \(Z (1 + \text{pathCutoff} (x)) t \|m\|\) and the weighted omitted-path bound is \(Ze^{- \sigma \text{pathCutoff} (x)} \|m\|\). The carry estimate gives \(\|m\| \leqslant Xt\). Consequently, with \(\eta = \min \{\tau, \sigma\}\), \(|P_r (V, m) - I_r (m) | \leqslant ZXt \hspace{0.17em} \text{widen}_x (t)\). Combining these inequalities proves, for a constant \(A_0\),

\(\displaystyle | \Delta_r | \leqslant A_0 t \hspace{0.17em} \text{widen}_x (t), \qquad d (o, r) \leqslant B / 2.\) (14.43)

This derivation uses the same frozen response and the same outer cutoff for every row; it does not reset the gauge or history panel by panel.

Normal residual and the mean. Let \(M\) denote the actual row operator, \(\Pi_0\) the projection onto inactive coordinates, \(A = M^{\ast} M + \Pi_0\), and let \(r_0\) be the affine source vector with \(\ell = M^{\ast} r_0\). Let \(\textsf{P}\) be the padded principal inverse used by the local Gaussian mean. For the frozen candidate \(a\), the selected affine residual is \(r (a) = Ma + r_0\); on each selected row its coordinate is the change \(\Delta_r\) estimated above. The exact normal residual identities are

\(\displaystyle \mu = - \textsf{P}^{\ast} \ell, \qquad \mu - a = E^{\ast} a - \textsf{P}^{\ast} (Aa + \ell), \qquad Aa + \ell = M^{\ast} r (a) + \Pi_0 a, \quad E = A \textsf{P} - I.\) (14.44)

These are the same finite-dimensional identities as (8.115). The candidate \(a\) is extended by zero outside the active coordinates, so \(\Pi_0 a = 0\). All queried mean coordinates are active; no assertion that every globally inactive coordinate is a tree coordinate is used. Each contributing row entry of \(M\) is bounded by a fixed constant \(M_0\), and at most \(D_x\) rows contribute. Equation (14.43) therefore gives

\(\displaystyle | (Aa + \ell)_j | \leqslant D_x M_0 A_0 t \hspace{0.17em} \text{widen}_x (t) .\)

The local inverse-column sum is bounded by \(N_0\). The inverse-window estimate is used only at the queried central coordinate: \(| (E^{\ast} a)_i | \leqslant E_0 e^{- sB} Xt\). No global operator-norm estimate for \(E\) is asserted. Since \(\|a\|_{\infty} \leqslant Xt\), (14.44) implies

\(\displaystyle | \mu_i - a_i | \leqslant N_0 D_x M_0 A_0 t \hspace{0.17em} \text{widen}_x (t) + E_0 e^{- sB} Xt.\) (14.45)

In the row-Gram formulation used for the actual reader, let \(Q\) be the row Gram matrix, \(b\) its affine source, and \(\nu_W = Q [W]^{- 1}\) extended by zero off the selected panel. The corresponding exact identity is

\(\displaystyle - \nu_W^{\ast} b - a = (Q \nu_W - I)^{\ast} a - \nu_W^{\ast} (Qa + b),\)

This form makes the local residual sum and the off-window tail separate.

The radius choice \(t \leqslant R \chi (x)\) gives \(t \leqslant C_R (1 + B)^5 \vartheta (x)\). Put \(b_x = 1 + B\) and \(\mathcal{B}_R (x) = 16 R 3^5 n_{\text{plaq}} b_x^5\); then \(R \chi (x) \leqslant \mathcal{B}_R (x) \vartheta (x)\). With \(u = x^{- 1 / 2 + 1 / 32}\), the temperature and cutoff bounds give \(t \leqslant \mathcal{B}_R (x) u\) and \(\text{widen}_x (t) \leqslant b_x \mathcal{B}_R (x) u + u\); both exponential tails are at most \(x^{- 1}\) eventually, since \(B = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil\) and \(\text{pathCutoff} (x)\) is a fixed positive fraction of \(B\) up to an additive constant. Thus fixed polynomials in \(1 + B\) are subpower in \(x\). The remaining factors are bounded by the explicit polynomial

\(\displaystyle Y (x) = N_0 D_x M_0 A_0 \mathcal{B}_R (x) (b_x \mathcal{B}_R (x) + 1) + E_0 X\mathcal{B}_R (x) .\)

The row count above gives \(D_x \leqslant Cb_x^4\), so the displayed polynomial satisfies \(Y (x) \leqslant C' (1 + B)^{15} \leqslant x^{1 / 16}\) eventually. Hence the right side of (14.45) is at most

\(\displaystyle Y (x) u^2 \leqslant x^{1 / 16} x^{- 1 + 1 / 16} = x^{- 7 / 8},\)

which proves (14.40). The cutoff and all row counts here are those at the outer radius \(B\).

The inverse-column comparison. The official-address map preserves physical anchors, row labels and the sector-membership test. Its restriction to a panel is onto: a bare address in the panel is within outer distance \(B\) of the cleared center, so the seed-dome exclusion makes it live in the actual store. Injectivity is immediate from the official map. This proves the finite index bijection only; it does not identify matrix entries.

For each matched pair of indices, the local row estimate gives an entrywise Gram bound. For each row constant \(A \geqslant 0\), define

\(\displaystyle w_x (A, t) = A (1 + \text{pathCutoff} (x)) t, \qquad \mathfrak{m}_x (A, t) = D_x ((A + w_x (A, t)) w_x (A, t) + w_x (A, t) A) .\)

Set \(w_x = w_x (A_1, t)\) and \(\mathfrak{m}_x =\mathfrak{m}_x (A_1, t)\); then the entrywise error is at most \(\delta_x =\mathfrak{m}_x\). The moving row differs by at most \(w_x\) and the identity row is at most \(A_1\). Thus, term by term,

\(\displaystyle |a_{rj} a_{rk} - b_{rj} b_{rk} | \leqslant |a_{rj} | \hspace{0.17em} |a_{rk} - b_{rk} | + |a_{rj} - b_{rj} | \hspace{0.17em} |b_{rk} | \leqslant (A_1 + w_x) w_x + w_x A_1 .\)

At most \(D_x\) rows contribute, proving the claimed entrywise bound \(\delta_x\). Let \(n_p\) bound the number of coordinates in either panel. For positive panel matrices with lower bounds \(\lambda\) and \(\Lambda\), the ordinary resolvent identity gives

\(\displaystyle X^{- 1} - Y^{- 1} = X^{- 1} (Y - X) Y^{- 1}, \qquad | (X^{- 1} - Y^{- 1})_{ij} | \leqslant n_p^2 \lambda^{- 1} \delta_x \Lambda^{- 1} .\)

This is precisely the finite-window comparison; its two distinct spectral floors are both used. The count \(n_p\) is bounded by the number of colors times the local jet-slot count at radius \(p\).

The nonnegative panel charge is

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{C}_x = n_p^2 \lambda^{- 1} \mathfrak{m}_x \Lambda^{- 1} .\)

The local stencil count gives \(n_p \leqslant Cb_x^4\) and \(D_x \leqslant Cb_x^4\). Also \(t \leqslant Cb_x^5 \vartheta (x)\) and \(w_x \leqslant Cb_x^6 \vartheta (x)\). Since \(\vartheta (x) \leqslant 1\) eventually, the definition of \(\mathfrak{m}_x\) yields \(\mathfrak{m}_x \leqslant Cb_x^{16} \vartheta (x)\) and hence \(\mathcal{C}_x \leqslant Cb_x^{24} \vartheta (x)\). The subpower scale bound implies \(Cb_x^{24} \leqslant x^{1 / 8}\) for all sufficiently large \(x\); the temperature bound is \(\vartheta (x) \leqslant x^{- 1 / 2 + 1 / 8} = x^{- 3 / 8}\). Their product is \(x^{- 1 / 4}\), proving (14.41). Equivalently, the fixed multiplier \(R\) can be included in the row constant: \(w_x (A, R \chi (x)) = w_x (RA, \chi (x))\) and \(A \leqslant RA\), so \(\mathfrak{m}_x (A, R \chi (x)) \leqslant \mathfrak{m}_x (RA, \chi (x))\).\(\Box\)

14.8Curvature observables under a common Gaussian law

This section records the nonlinear step between a local Gaussian reader and the group-valued field that it is meant to read. All comparisons use one coarse field, one gauge based at one site, one parent forecast, and one joint Gaussian sample. A patch restricts which addresses are tested; it does not change the forecast, covariance, or gauge from address to address. The local Gaussian hypotheses used here are those of Subsection 14.10. That section's same-pose mean and covariance estimates remain inputs, as do the paid regularity and window hypotheses specified below.

Let \(x\) be the large coarse parameter, \(L\) the fixed blocking side, and \(w\) the fixed width multiplier of the fine read. Set

\(\displaystyle y = x + c_L, \qquad R = x^{- 3 / 8}, \qquad g = \beta^{- 1 / 2}, \qquad g' = (\beta')^{- 1 / 2} .\)

Here \(c_L \geqslant 0\) is the fixed scale shift. The coupling band is \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta \leqslant 3 x\), \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta'\) and \(| \beta' - \beta | \leqslant \gamma\), for a fixed \(\gamma > 0\). In particular \(\sqrt{x / 2} \leqslant g^{- 1} \leqslant \sqrt{3 x}\). Define

\(\displaystyle \text{phase} (v) = v^{- 1 / 2} \exp ((\log \log v)^{3 / 2}), \qquad \tau = \text{phase} (x), \qquad t = \text{phase} (y) .\)

Set \(B (v) = \lceil (\log v)^2 \rceil\) and \(W_f = wB (y)^2\). The recording parameter \(r (x) \in [x, x^2]\) has temperature \(\tau\). Thus the logarithmic clearance allowance from Subsection 14.7 on this recording scale is

\(\displaystyle a_x = 16 \{2 (B (r (x)) + 1) + 1\}^5 n_{\text{plaq}} \hspace{0.17em} \tau .\)

Fix the coarse chart constant \(C_{\text{core}}\) and a common constant \(C_0 \geqslant 1\) such that the deterministic link response to a direction of size at most \(a_x\) is bounded by \(C_0 a_x\) on the funded tube. Let \(C_{\text{high}}\) and \(A_{\ast}\) be the chord and curvature constants for the two-exponential chart estimate proved below. Let \(n_G\) be the real dimension of the gauge algebra and put \(e = (A_{\ast} + n_G) R^2\). All these constants are fixed before taking large \(x\). Let \(\mathcal{C}_o (V)_q\) denote the nonlinear plaquette-curvature coordinate of the coarse field in the fixed gauge based at \(o\), indexed by coarse plaquettes and color coordinates. The parent forecast is the fixed real row

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{P}_{o, V, g'} (u, a) = \frac{1}{g'} \sum_q K_{u, a ; q} \hspace{0.17em} \mathcal{C}_o (V)_q .\)

Let \(D_{A, F, x}\) denote the linear response on the selected store \(A\) and forest \(F\). It inserts the active coordinates of the joint Gaussian vector into the fine link field, with zeros off the active coordinates, then takes the signed curl and reads its real color coordinates in the fixed frame. For a joint Gaussian vector \(Z\) we write

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{G}_{A, F, x} (Z) = D_{A, F, x} Z\)

for the resulting zero-padded signed-curl response. When an incidence index \(j\) is used instead of \((u, a)\), it denotes that same plaquette/color row. The same \(V, o, g'\) and kernel \(K\) are used for every tested row. In particular, \(\mathcal{P}\) is not recomputed from a patch-dependent field.

14.8.1The scalar scale and ticket estimates

Fix a patch of side \(L\) and a finite transition tube with its row and chart bounds. For the positive cut rate \(s\) and nonnegative strip parameter \(z\) of this tube, put

\(\displaystyle m_2 = \binom{4}{2} = 6, \qquad M_0 = \frac{m_2^2 z^3}{L^2}, \qquad P_0 = m_2 M_0 .\)

The two diagonal installations and the transport row give the cubic strip bound \(M_0\), as in Subsection 14.6. The factor \(m_2\) in \(P_0\) sums over two-form output labels. For fixed \(z\) choose \(L\) large enough that \(2 n_G P_0 \leqslant 1 / 4096\), which follows from \(P_0 = m_2^3 z^3 / L^2\). Define the row-tail and comparison bounds

\(\displaystyle h_x = \left\lfloor \frac{B (x)}{10000} \right\rfloor, \quad \Delta (x) = P_0 e^{- sh_x}, \quad E (x) = C_{\text{core}} (\Delta (x) R + P_0 R^2) .\)

The row and chart estimates to which these bounds are applied are specified in Lemma 14.8.

Since \(r (x) \leqslant x^2\), the logarithmic factor in \(a_x\) is bounded by a fixed power of \(1 + \log x\). Also \(\tau = x^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\), \(t \geqslant y^{- 1 / 2}\), and eventually \(t \leqslant 2 x^{- 7 / 16}\). The phase ratio \(\tau / t \leqslant \sqrt{y / x} \leqslant 2\) follows for large \(x\) from the monotonicity of the exponential factor on \(y \geqslant x\). For every fixed chart radius \(r_G > 0\), these bounds and \(R = x^{- 3 / 8}\) give, eventually,

\(\displaystyle 4 C_0 a_x \leqslant R \leqslant r_G, \qquad x^{- 7 / 8} \leqslant R^2, \qquad \Delta (x) \leqslant R.\) (14.46)

For the last inequality use \(h_x \geqslant (\log x)^2 / 10000 - 1\) and \(s > 0\). In particular \(E (x) \leqslant C_{\text{core}} (1 + P_0) R^2\). Uniformly in the coupling band,

\(\displaystyle |g / g' - 1| = \frac{| \beta' - \beta |}{\sqrt{\beta} (\sqrt{\beta'} + \sqrt{\beta})} \leqslant \gamma / x \leqslant x^{- 1 / 8} .\)

Since \(E (x) / g = O (x^{- 1 / 4})\), \(g^{- 1} = O (\sqrt{x})\) and \(\tau = x^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\), both terms of the following quotient are \(o (x^{- 1 / 16})\), uniformly in the coupling band. Hence

\(\displaystyle \frac{E (x) + P_0 \tau x^{- 1 / 8}}{g} \leqslant x^{- 1 / 16}\) (14.47)

eventually. The vector bound is also eventual. Indeed \(n_G P_0 \tau \leqslant t / 4096\) by the choice of \(L\), and \(n_G E (x) \leqslant t / 4096\) for large \(x\) because \(R^2 = o (t)\).

Write \(c_G\) for the color-scale constant in the curvature product estimate below and \(\delta_G\) for the radius of the local curvature lower bound. Use \(M = C_{\text{high}} R\), and for positive \(g\) and any room radius \(W\) define

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{N}_G (M, g, W) = \frac{c_G \{u (4 M) + (u + 4 M) u + 6 M^2 \}}{g}, \qquad u = 4 WM.\) (14.48)

Subsubsection 14.8.4 derives this expression from the based holonomy and the four-link product. For fixed \(w\), \(W_f^2 = O ((1 + B (x))^4)\) because \(y = x + c_L\). Thus \(\mathcal{N}_G (M, g, W_f) = O ((1 + B (x))^4 x^{- 1 / 4}) = o (x^{- 1 / 8})\). Together with \(R^2 = o (t)\) and \(e / g = O (x^{- 1 / 4})\), this gives the remaining large-\(x\) comparisons

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} 4 M & \leqslant \delta_G, \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.49)} \\ 6 c_G M^2 + 4 e & \leqslant t / 64, \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.50)} \\ \mathcal{N}_G (M, g, W_f) + 4 e / g & \leqslant x^{- 1 / 8} . \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.51)} \end{array}\)

We use the local curvature estimate (8.71). With the group-dependent radius \(\delta_G\) and \(\text{chord} (U) \leqslant \delta_G\), it gives

\(\displaystyle \frac{3}{4} \hspace{0.17em} \text{chord} (U)^2 \leqslant \| \kappa_G (U)\|^2 .\)

The constants in this inequality depend only on the fixed compact gauge group. All large-\(x\) thresholds here are for fixed \(L\), patch, and transition tube with the stated bounds.

Lemma 14.7. (Eventual local room) For fixed \(G, L, w, z, s, \gamma\) as above and a transition tube with the stated paid chart bounds, all sufficiently large \(x\) and all \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta \leqslant 3 x\), \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta'\) and \(| \beta' - \beta | \leqslant \gamma\) have the following properties. Set \(\kappa_0 = 1 / 4096\).

  1. the chart clearance is at most \(R\), \(R\) lies below the fixed chart margin, and \(x^{- 7 / 8} \leqslant R^2\);

  2. for every coarse direction of norm at most the clearance, the sum of the deterministic response bound, \(n_G x^{- 7 / 8}\) and the Gaussian reserve \(\kappa_0 t\) is at most \(R\);

  3. the near-identity link bound is at most \(C_{\text{high}} R\), and the four-face curvature budget is at most \(t / 64\);

  4. the sum of the four-face gauge-reading error, the coupling error and the residual curvature bound is at most \(x^{- 1 / 32}\).

The assertions hold uniformly in the period for the fixed patch and local data.

Proof. The deterministic response has size at most \(C_0 a_x \leqslant R / 4\) by (14.46). Since \(x^{- 7 / 8} / R = x^{- 1 / 2}\) and \(t / R \leqslant 2 x^{- 1 / 16}\), the color-count and reserve terms are each at most \(R / 4\) after increasing \(x_0\). This proves the room estimate. The link bound is the fixed chart constant times \(R\); the four-face budget is \(O (R^2) = O (x^{- 3 / 4})\), while \(t \geqslant y^{- 1 / 2} \geqslant cx^{- 1 / 2}\) for large \(x\), proving the curvature budget. Finally, the definition of the four-face gauge error is

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{N}_G (C_{\text{high}} R, g, W_f) + 4 e / g.\)

The scalar scale estimate bounds this by \(x^{- 1 / 8}\). Adding the paid row read error \(x^{- 1 / 16}\) and the remaining coupling allowance gives at most \(x^{- 1 / 32}\) eventually, since each stronger negative power divided by \(x^{- 1 / 32}\) tends to zero. The fixed costs were chosen before the period, so the threshold is period-independent.\(\Box\)

14.8.2From the coarse forecast to a fine curvature row

Let the coarse side be \(c\) and the fine side be \(f\), with a fixed blocking map. Let \(V\) be the coarse field and \(o\) a single base site. The common pose assumption is that one based gauge transforms \(V\) on the whole room about \(o\) into the exponential of one real direction \(v\), with \(\|v\|_{\infty} \leqslant a_x / 2\), where the norm of a link field is the maximum of its Euclidean color norms. The field is healthy at threshold \(\tau\) on that room. The curvature coordinate has norm at most the plaquette chord by (8.70), hence at most \(\tau\) there. The chosen stores have no support there. The same gauge is used for every row and plaquette below.

Let \(F\) be the selected forest and \(Z\) the actual joint Gaussian sample on its active link coordinates. Write \(z_j\) for its insertion into the fine link field (zero outside the active coordinates). The stationary linear fine response is \(\ell =\mathcal{U}_F v\) from Subsection 8.14, using the same tube and forest. On an internal bond set \(b_j = 0\). On a crossing bond set \(b_j\) equal to the corresponding coarse direction of \(v\). The replica link is the identity in the first case and copies the coarse link in the second. Because the pose gives \(V^o = \exp v\) at the relevant coarse bonds in the gauge based at \(o\), the replica link equals \(\exp b_j\) in both cases, with \(\|b_j \| \leqslant \|v\|_{\infty} \leqslant R\). The stationary relative response of Subsection 8.14 is \(d_j = \ell_j - b_j\). This is the decomposition of the tangent in the replica coordinates: differentiating \(\exp (\epsilon d_j) \exp (\epsilon b_j)\) at \(\epsilon = 0\) gives \(d_j + b_j = \ell_j\). The comparison mean \(m^0\) in (14.67) is the restriction of this \(d\) to the active bonds in the same forest.

Let \(w_j\) be the actual local mean in those relative coordinates and put \(r_j = gz_j\). The pointwise mean estimate (14.67) at recording scale \(r (x) \geqslant x\) bounds each color component of \(w_j - d_j\) by \(x^{- 7 / 8}\) on the interior active bonds. The Euclidean norm is bounded by the sum of component moduli, so \(\|w_j - d_j \| \leqslant n_G x^{- 7 / 8}\). The cleared stencils place every queried nonforest bond in the active set. The update first uses the link increment \(\exp (w_j + r_j)\) and then the replica link. On forest bonds the update and the replica are both the identity, so the relative coordinates \(w_j, d_j, r_j\) there are zero. Thus the actual fine field on each of these bonds is

\(\displaystyle U_j = \exp (w_j + r_j) \exp (b_j), \qquad \ell_j = d_j + b_j .\) (14.52)

Write \(\textsf{M}\) for the fixed unitary representation and \(\Pi_{\mathfrak{g}}\) for the real-linear color projection. In the physical normalization its operator norm for the Frobenius norm is at most \(1 / c_G\), while \(\| \textsf{M} (U) - I\|_F = c_G \text{chord} (U)\). The curvature coordinate is

\(\displaystyle \kappa_G (U) = \Pi_{\mathfrak{g}} \hspace{-0.17em} \left( \frac{\textsf{M} (U) - \textsf{M} (U^{- 1})}{2} \right) = \Pi_{\mathfrak{g}} (\textsf{M} (U) - I) .\)

Here \(\textsf{M} (U^{- 1}) = \textsf{M} (U)^{\ast}\), so removing the Hermitian part does not affect the projection onto the skew-Hermitian Lie algebra. It follows by expanding the product of the matrix increments that

\(\displaystyle \kappa_G (UV) - \kappa_G (U) - \kappa_G (V) = \Pi_{\mathfrak{g}} ((\textsf{M} (U) - I) (\textsf{M} (V) - I)) .\) (14.53)

The Frobenius product inequality bounds its norm by \(c_G \text{chord} (U) \text{chord} (V)\).

Choose \(r_G > 0\) so that the exponential and its curvature coordinate have bounded second derivative on the closed ball of radius \(r_G\). The matrix exponential is smooth, and its derivative at zero is the inclusion of the Lie algebra in the matrix space. Projecting this inclusion back to color space is the identity. Taylor's formula on radial segments therefore gives constants \(C_{\exp}, A_{\exp} > 0\) depending only on the gauge chart such that

\(\displaystyle \text{chord} (\exp a) \leqslant C_{\exp} \|a\|, \qquad \| \kappa_G (\exp a) - a\| \leqslant A_{\exp} \|a\|^2 \quad (\|a\| \leqslant r_G) .\)

For \(\|a\|, \|b\| \leqslant R \leqslant r_G\), chord subadditivity bounds the chord of \(\exp a \exp b\) by \(2 C_{\exp} R\). Equation (14.53) and the two radial estimates bound the curvature error from \(a + b\) by \((c_G C_{\exp}^2 + 2 A_{\exp}) R^2\). Thus one may take \(C_{\text{high}} = 2 C_{\exp}\) and \(A_{\ast} = c_G C_{\exp}^2 + 2 A_{\exp}\) in the scalar estimates. Any increase of these fixed constants preserves the same eventual powers.

The guarded event is

\(\displaystyle \|gz_j \| \leqslant \kappa_0 t\)

for every bond in the enlarged room. The paid room estimate is

\(\displaystyle \|w_j + r_j \| \leqslant \|d_j \| + n_G x^{- 7 / 8} + \kappa_0 t \leqslant R,\)

and we have shown \(\|b_j \| \leqslant R\). Apply the two-exponential chart bound to \((w_j + r_j, b_j)\) to obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} \text{chord} (U_j) & \leqslant C_{\text{high}} R, \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.54)} \\ \| \kappa_G (U_j) - (\ell_j + r_j)\| & \leqslant e. \hspace{3cm} \text{(14.55)} \end{array}\)

Indeed, \(w_j + r_j + b_j = (d_j + b_j) + r_j + (w_j - d_j) = \ell_j + r_j + (w_j - d_j)\). The portrait term is at most \(n_G x^{- 7 / 8}\); since both Taylor arguments have norm at most \(R\), the two-link curvature remainder is at most \(A_{\ast} R^2\). Since \(x^{- 7 / 8} \leqslant R^2\), their sum is bounded by \(e = (A_{\ast} + n_G) R^2\). Here \(\ell_j + gz_j\) is the value of the link direction at the single bond \(j\); the face boundary sum is taken only in the next subsection.

Lemma 14.8. (Read error against the common parent forecast) Assume the paid row and transport estimates for the selected transition tube: on every readable address in the room, the fine response row differs from its shorter parent row by total absolute mass at most \(\Delta\), the parent row has mass at most \(P_0\), and the deterministic coarse curvature Taylor bounds stated below hold. Let \(\Delta = m_2 M_0 e^{- sh_x}\), the absolute tail bound for the two-form row with strip bound \(M_0\). Then for every interior fine plaquette \(u\) and color coordinate \(a\), with \(f = \text{faceCurl} (u, \ell)\),

\(\displaystyle \|f\| \leqslant t / 2048, \qquad | g^{- 1} f_a -\mathcal{P}_{o, V, g'} (u, a) | \leqslant x^{- 1 / 16} .\) (14.56)

Proof. Index coarse plaquette/color coordinates by \(q\). Let \(m_q\) be the linearized coarse plaquette curvature in direction \(v\), and let \((u_0)_q =\mathcal{C}_o (V)_q\) be the nonlinear curvature in the same based gauge. The exact response identities are

\(\displaystyle f_a = \sum_q P_{u, a ; q} m_q, \qquad Q_{u, a} = \sum_q K_{u, a ; q} (u_0)_q, \qquad \mathcal{P}_{o, V, g'} (u, a) = Q_{u, a} / g' .\)

The deterministic chart estimates give the global linear-row bound \(|m_q | \leqslant C_{\text{core}} R\) for every tested coarse plaquette/color coordinate. On the support of the parent row \(K_{u, a ; \cdot}\) they also give \(|m_q - (u_0)_q | \leqslant C_{\text{core}} R^2\). The purchased row bounds are

\(\displaystyle \sum_q |P_{u, a ; q} - K_{u, a ; q} | \leqslant \Delta, \qquad \sum_q |K_{u, a ; q} | \leqslant P_0 .\)

Subtract the response sums and add/subtract \(\sum_q K_{u, a ; q} m_q\). Then

\(\displaystyle |f_a - Q_{u, a} | \leqslant C_{\text{core}} R \sum_q |P_{u, a ; q} - K_{u, a ; q} | + C_{\text{core}} R^2 \sum_q |K_{u, a ; q} | \leqslant \Delta C_{\text{core}} R + P_0 C_{\text{core}} R^2 = E (x) .\)

For the normalized comparison, \(|Q_{u, a} | \leqslant P_0 \tau\) since the nonlinear parent curvature coordinates have modulus at most \(\tau\). Thus

\(\displaystyle \left| \frac{f_a}{g} - \frac{Q_{u, a}}{g'} \right| \leqslant \frac{|f_a - Q_{u, a} | + |Q_{u, a} | \hspace{0.17em} {|g'}^{- 1} g - 1|}{g} \leqslant \frac{E (x) + P_0 \tau x^{- 1 / 8}}{g} \leqslant x^{- 1 / 16},\)

by the coupling bound and (14.47). For the vector bound, sum the component bounds to get \(\|f\| \leqslant n_G (P_0 \tau + E (x)) \leqslant t / 2048\) by the scalar estimates above. All rows have finite support in the selected window, and the bounds are period-uniform.\(\Box\)

14.8.3The four-bond curvature and the common gauge read

For a fine plaquette \(u\), order its bonds \(j_1, j_2, j_3, j_4\) so that the plaquette product is

\(\displaystyle \Xi_u = U_{j_1} U_{j_2} U_{j_3}^{- 1} U_{j_4}^{- 1} .\)

For a link field \(A\), let \(\text{faceCurl}_u (A)\) be the signed sum of the four link coordinates in this order. Apply the two-factor relation above successively to the four oriented factors \(U_{j_1}, U_{j_2}, U_{j_3}^{- 1}, U_{j_4}^{- 1}\). Each has chord at most \(M = C_{\text{high}} R\), and inversion preserves chord while negating curvature. At the second factor the defect is at most \(c_G M^2\). At the third, the preceding product has chord at most \(2 M\), so the new defect is at most \(2 c_G M^2\). At the fourth, its chord is at most \(3 M\), so the new defect is at most \(3 c_G M^2\). Summing gives exactly \((1 + 2 + 3) c_G M^2 = 6 c_G M^2\). Replacing the four individual link-curvature coordinates by their linear directions using (14.55) costs at most \(4 e\). Consequently

\(\displaystyle \| \kappa_G (\Xi_u) - \text{faceCurl}_u (\ell + gz)\| \leqslant 6 c_G (C_{\text{high}} R)^2 + 4 e \leqslant t / 64,\) (14.57)

by (14.50). Linearity of the face curl, the bound from Lemma 14.8, and the four guarded link increments give

\(\displaystyle \| \text{faceCurl}_u (\ell + gz)\| \leqslant t / 2048 + 4 \kappa_0 t.\)

Consequently the product curvature coordinate has norm at most \(t / 2048 + 4 \kappa_0 t + t / 64 < t / 32\). Since \(\kappa_0 = 1 / 4096\),

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{2048} + \frac{4}{4096} + \frac{1}{64} < \frac{1}{32} .\)

The plaquette chord is at most four times the link chord, so \(4 C_{\text{high}} R \leqslant \delta_G\) keeps it inside the local curvature chart. The local lower bound \(\tfrac{3}{4} \text{chord} (\Xi_u)^2 \leqslant \| \kappa_G (\Xi_u)\|^2\) therefore gives

\(\displaystyle \text{chord} (\Xi_u) \leqslant \frac{2 t}{32 \sqrt{3}} < t / 8.\) (14.58)

Thus if the health flag threshold is the phase \(t\), the tested flag is strictly below threshold. Applying this to each plaquette in the tested patch proves the healthy-mask conclusion. It is a local conclusion on the guarded sample; all plaquettes used in this deduction lie in the enlarged room.

14.8.4Based transport for the fine read

Let \(r\) be the read center on the fine torus and \(v_0\) the base vertex of the tested plaquette \(u\). The read is in the based gauge obtained by transporting along the fixed coordinate paths from \(r\). If the fine period is \(n\), the condition \(2 (W_f + 1) < n\) gives an unwrapped coordinate box of radius \(W_f\) about \(r\). When the plaquette is at least two steps inside this box, its four bonds and the coordinate path to \(v_0\) lie in it. The latter path has at most \(4 W_f\) steps since each of the four displacements is at most \(W_f\). Write \(H\) for its ordered product in \(U\). By the chord triangle and inverse identities, \(\text{chord} (H) \leqslant 4 W_f M\) and \(\text{chord} (\Xi_u) \leqslant 4 M\).

For a site gauge \(k\) sending \(U'\) to \(U\), multiplying the gauge-transformed links along the path cancels the gauge factors at all intermediate sites. The path product transforms by \(k (r)\) at the start and \(k (v_0)^{- 1}\) at the end. At the plaquette the factors telescope to conjugation by \(k (v_0)\). Hence the based plaquette in \(U\) is the based plaquette in \(U'\) conjugated by \(k (r)\). The curvature projection is equivariant under conjugation, since the adjoint action of the unitary representation preserves the Lie subspace and its orthogonal complement. It follows that the normalized curvature read in \(U'\) after the color rotation by \(k (r)\) equals \(g^{- 1} \kappa_G (H \Xi_u H^{- 1})\) in \(U\).

Apply (14.53) at \((H, \Xi_u)\) and \((H \Xi_u, H^{- 1})\). The terms \(\kappa_G (H)\) and \(\kappa_G (H^{- 1})\) cancel. With \(u_0 = 4 W_f M\), the two defects give

\(\displaystyle \| \kappa_G (H \Xi_u H^{- 1}) - \kappa_G (\Xi_u)\| \leqslant c_G \{u_0 (4 M) + (u_0 + 4 M) u_0 \} .\)

Before division by \(g\), the difference between \(\kappa_G (\Xi_u)\) and the signed sum of the four bond curvature coordinates is bounded by \(6 c_G M^2\), as proved above. This yields exactly \(\mathcal{N}_G (M, g, W_f)\) in (14.48) as an upper bound for the discrepancy between the rotated read and the normalized signed sum. Replacing each bond curvature by \(\ell_j + gz_j\) costs at most \(e / g\) per bond by (14.55). The signed sum then reads \(g^{- 1} f_a +\mathcal{G}_{A, F, x} (Z) (u, a)\) in color coordinate \(a\), by linearity and the definition of \(D_{A, F, x}\). Finally (14.56) replaces \(g^{- 1} f_a\) by the common parent forecast with error \(x^{- 1 / 16}\). The total is at most

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{N}_G (M, g, W_f) + 4 e / g + x^{- 1 / 16} \leqslant x^{- 1 / 8} + x^{- 1 / 16} \leqslant x^{- 1 / 32}\)

for large \(x\). The gauge used to compare \(U'\) to \(U\) can vary. The within-room forecast, sample, and coarse pose are fixed throughout.

Proposition 14.9. (Actual-reader curvature and read estimate) Fix a selected transition tube and one posed coarse field with based gauge at \(o\). Suppose the actual Gaussian reader estimates of Subsection 14.10, the paid row and chart bounds above, and the local window/separation conditions hold, including \(2 (W_f + 1) < n\) for the fine period \(n\). For each sample in the enlarged room with \(\|gz_j \| \leqslant \kappa_0 t\) on every active bond, the resulting fine field is healthy on the selected patch. For every gauge-equivalent fine field and every incidence row \(j = (u, a)\) whose two-step neighborhood lies inside the patch, its rigid color read differs from the same parent forecast plus the transported Gaussian curvature by at most \(x^{- 1 / 32}\). All conclusions are uniform in the period once the displayed fixed-data threshold is reached.

Proof. Use (14.52) for all bonds in the enlarged room, then apply Lemma 14.8 to each tested row. Equation (14.58) gives the health estimate. The based-transport calculation and (14.51) give the read estimate. All statements use the same base site and parent forecast, and their constants do not depend on the number of sites outside the tested patch.\(\Box\)

14.8.5The lost-mask estimate for a correlated Gaussian

Let \(\mathcal{I}_y\) be the actual finite set of real link-color coordinates read by the guard on the enlarged room. Its cardinality satisfies

\(\displaystyle m_y := |\mathcal{I}_y | \leqslant C_w (\log y)^{16}\) (14.59)

for a constant \(C_w\) depending on the fixed width and color dimension, but not on the period. Indeed the room radius for the mask is \(W_f + 8\), \(B (y)^2 \leqslant 4 (\log y)^4\) for large \(y\), and the number of link-color coordinates in a four-dimensional box is at most a fixed multiple of its side to the fourth power.

If \(m_y = 0\), the product veil is \(1\) and the loss is zero. Otherwise assume \(m_y \geqslant 1\); the gauge algebra is nontrivial, so \(n_G \geqslant 1\). Group the coordinates by bond, writing \(X_b \in \mathfrak{g}\) for the actual Lie-valued fluctuation read on bond \(b\). Let \(X = (X_i)_{i \in \mathcal{I}_y}\) have the actual centered joint Gaussian law. Its covariance matrix is positive semidefinite and each entry has absolute value at most \(U\), where \(U \geqslant 1\) is the uniform matrix bound from the actual reader estimates. For \(v > 0\), each marginal obeys the Chernoff bound

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} (|X_i | \geqslant v) \leqslant 2 \exp (- v^2 / (2 U)) ;\)

this follows by optimizing \(\mathbb{E}e^{sX_i} = e^{s^2 \text{Var} (X_i) / 2}\) at \(s = v / U\) and applying the same estimate to \(- X_i\). A union bound, which does not assume independence, gives

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} \{\max_i |X_i | \geqslant v\} \leqslant 2 m_y \exp (- v^2 / (2 U)) .\)

For the bondwise guard, consider the event \(\max_b \|X_b \| \geqslant v\). On this event one of the \(n_G\) real coordinates of some \(X_b\) has modulus at least \(v / \sqrt{n_G}\). A union bound therefore gives

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} \{\max_b \|X_b \| \geqslant v\} \leqslant 2 m_y \exp (- v^2 / (2 n_G U)) .\)

Set \(\varrho_y = [8 n_G (1 + m_y U)]^{- 1}\). Then \(1 / (2 n_G U) - \varrho_y \geqslant 3 / (8 n_G U)\). The guard threshold grows faster than every power of \(\log y\), so eventually \(3 v^2 / (8 n_G U) \geqslant \log (2 m_y) - 2\). Hence

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} \{\max_b \|X_b \| \geqslant v\} \leqslant e^2 \exp (- \varrho_y v^2) .\) (14.60)

The vector is the actual joint Gaussian. Correlation is allowed throughout; only its marginal variance bound and the finite union bound are used.

Take the guard width

\(\displaystyle v = \frac{\kappa_0 t}{2 g} .\)

For the band \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta \leqslant 3 x\), the scalar coupling bound gives \(v \geqslant \delta_0 \hspace{0.17em} \text{hot} (y)\) with a fixed \(\delta_0 > 0\); here \(\text{hot} (y) = \exp ((\log \log y)^{3 / 2})\). By (14.59),

\(\displaystyle 1 + m_y U \leqslant (1 + C_w U) (\log y)^{16} .\)

For every fixed \(p \geqslant 0\), the ratio

\(\displaystyle \frac{\delta_0^2 \exp (2 (\log \log y)^{3 / 2})}{8 n_G (1 + C_w U) (\log y)^{16} (2 + p) \log y} \longrightarrow \infty .\)

Indeed, writing \(q = \log \log y\), the logarithm of the numerator growth relative to the denominator is \(2 q^{3 / 2} - 17 q + O (1)\), which tends to \(+ \infty\). Thus eventually \(\varrho_y v^2 \geqslant (2 + p) \log y\), and (14.60) gives

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} \{\max_b \|X_b \| \geqslant v\} \leqslant e^2 y^{- (2 + p)} \leqslant x^{- p} .\) (14.61)

Let \(\Gamma\) be the smooth cutoff on the actual Gaussian vector: one radial factor for each active bond in the enlarged room. Each factor lies in \([0, 1]\), vanishes outside the vector guard radius \(\kappa_0 t / g\), and equals one on the ball of half that radius. Consequently

\(\displaystyle 0 \leqslant 1 - \Gamma (X) \leqslant \boldsymbol{1}_{\{\max_b \|X_b \| \geqslant v\}} .\)

Integrating and using (14.61) proves

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [1 - \Gamma (X)] \leqslant x^{- p} .\)

The estimate uses the marginal variances of the actual correlated Gaussian on its finite room.

Lemma 14.10. (One-patch Gaussian guard) For every fixed \(p \geqslant 0\), fixed patch width, and common palette entry bound \(U \geqslant 1\), the mask loss on the actual joint Gaussian sample is at most \(x^{- p}\) for all sufficiently large \(x\), uniformly in the period. In particular, choose \(p = 1\) to obtain the mask loss \(x^{- 1}\) used in the reader purchase.

Proof. The coordinate count is (14.59); the one-coordinate Chernoff estimate, union bound and scale comparison establish (14.61). The pointwise cutoff comparison then proves the expected mask loss.\(\Box\)

Remark 14.11. (Boundary of this package) The result proves the nonlinear curvature and read estimates and the power-small loss of the Gaussian cutoff for the fixed posed reader, under the stated Gaussian, chart, row and window hypotheses. The assertion that the warning factor vanishes for every compatible field/store palette requires the separate hypothesis of Subsection 14.3.

14.9Uniform Gaussian comparison on a coupling interval

We now put the local estimates on one selected history. The choices must be uniform in the finite period, the reader patch, the forest and the tag. A further distinction is essential: the temperature used to record a Gaussian sector need not be the physical coupling at which the field is sampled. We keep these parameters separate.

14.9.1One allowance for the whole coupling band

Fix a blocking patch with side \(L\), its predictor kernel and adjacent path data. Fix positive strip width \(s\) and lower metric bound \(a\), an upper bound \(z \geqslant 0\), a reader-room multiplier \(w \in \mathbb{N}\), a real error exponent \(f\), and a real coupling-drift bound \(\gamma\). The preselected block side must satisfy the predictor smallness condition

\(\displaystyle 2 \dim (\mathfrak{g}) \hspace{0.17em} T_{\Psi} (z, L) \leqslant 1 / 4096,\)

where \(T_{\Psi} (z, L)\) is the absolute-row ticket for the truncated coarse-to-fine curvature transport. This is a condition on the previous choice of \(L\), not something achieved by increasing the entrance coupling afterward.

For \(t > 1\) define

\(\displaystyle \ell (t) = \log \log t, \qquad \varepsilon_f (t) = \exp \{- f \ell (t)^2 \} .\)

For every fixed \(v > 0\) and real \(c\), eventually in \(t\),

\(\displaystyle x^{- v} \leqslant \varepsilon_c (t) \qquad (t / 2 \leqslant x \leqslant t) .\) (14.62)

In fact \(x^{- v} \leqslant 2^v t^{- v}\), and \(c (\log \log t)^2 = o (\log t)\). Taking logarithms proves the inequality, uniformly over the displayed interval. This elementary comparison lets us use one error allowance at the top of a plateau.

Here are the local inputs to which we apply it. The local Gaussian comparison gives one entry bound \(U \geqslant 1\), followed by an eventual threshold for its mean and covariance estimates. The nonlinear guarded comparison supplies its field estimates for all \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta \leqslant 3 x\), and the guard bound gives lost mass at most \(x^{- 1}\). The common covariance-cover and transport estimates supply both short-row errors at most \(x^{- 1 / 8}\) as follows. Their payments have the forms \(C_1 e^{- sh_x}\) and \(C_2 e^{- th_x}\), with \(s, t > 0\) and \(C_1, C_2\) fixed before \(x\), uniformly over periods and beds in the purchased strip class. Here \(h_x = \lfloor \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil / 10000 \rfloor \geqslant (\log x)^2 / 10000 - 1\). For any fixed \(C, c > 0\),

\(\displaystyle \log (x^{1 / 8} Ce^{- ch_x}) \leqslant \log C + c + \tfrac{1}{8} \log x - \tfrac{c}{10000} (\log x)^2 \longrightarrow - \infty .\)

Apply this to both payments and enlarge one common threshold. The adjacent coupling comparison holds uniformly for

\(\displaystyle x / 2 \leqslant \beta \leqslant 3 x, \qquad x / 2 \leqslant \beta', \qquad | \beta' - \beta | \leqslant \gamma .\) (14.63)

There is no independent upper restriction on \(\beta'\). Finally, the local chart thresholds give the room and logarithm inequalities used in the next subsection. These are respectively the conclusions of Sections 14.10, 14.8 and 14.6, with their stated same-data and prior-purchase hypotheses. Their production is a prerequisite here; the following choice argument does not supply an estimate absent from one of them.

Take the maximum of their finitely many thresholds, after \(L\) and the other displayed fixed parameters have been chosen. For all sufficiently large \(t\), every \(x \in [t / 2, t]\) is above that maximum. Set

\(\displaystyle p_x = x^{- 1 / 8} .\)

This value is chosen before \(\beta, \beta'\) and pays the short-row errors for every coupling pair in (14.63). Increase the common threshold once more so that

\(\displaystyle x^{- 1 / 8} + p_x \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t), \qquad x^{- 1 / 32} \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t), \qquad x^{- 1} \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t)^2 \quad (t / 2 \leqslant x \leqslant t) .\) (14.64)

For the first inequality apply (14.62) with \(c = f + 1\) and note that \(2 \varepsilon_{f + 1} (t) \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t)\) eventually. For the second use \(c = f, v = 1 / 32\); for the third use \(c = 2 f, v = 1\) and \(\varepsilon_{2 f} = \varepsilon_f^2\).

Thus one \(U\) is chosen first, then one entrance threshold, then for each \(x\) one \(p_x\), and all the estimates hold for every admissible coupling pair, period, forest and tag. No maximum over an infinite collection of periods is being taken: each input estimate already has its uniform threshold.

14.9.2The tube and covariance belong to the retained history

Let \(C\) be the selected family and fix its actual precision horizon and path attachment. At an adjacent step \(i < N\), form the local Gaussian data from the fine path at \(i + 1\), the coarse path at \(i\), their prescribed clips, and the tower between those very levels. The path bounds come from the Wilson representations of \(C\); the metric floors, path attachments, symmetry and crossing identities come from this horizon. They are therefore the same objects in the local Gaussian estimate and in the short-covariance construction. Replacing either by another witness with similar bounds would not justify the calculation below.

Suppose the step is in a constant-order plateau, \(J (i + 1) = J (i)\). Its strip width, metric floor and bound then have the same stage index on both sides. Write \(P_i^{\text{cond}}\) for its conditional curvature covariance, and \(Q_i^{\text{short}}\) for the prescribed finite-range covariance used in the reader expansion. The common-cover estimate, applied to the actual fine metric, gives

\(\displaystyle |P_i^{\text{cond}} (a, b) - Q_i^{\text{short}} (a, b) | \leqslant p_x .\) (14.65)

This includes the two cases in the short-row rule. Where the rule keeps the covariance entry there is no discrepancy. Where it sets an entry to zero, the identically truncated cover row has zero at that periodic address, so its tail estimate bounds the original entry. All images in the periodization are included in that support test.

For a posed actual sector, the local estimate gives \(|C_i^{\text{read}} (a, b) - P_i^{\text{cond}} (a, b) | \leqslant x^{- 1 / 8}\) on both reader supports. Adding (14.65) and using (14.64) yields

\(\displaystyle |C_i^{\text{read}} (a, b) - Q_i^{\text{short}} (a, b) | \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t) .\) (14.66)

The short covariance is a comparator. The random vector continues to have the actual sector covariance \(C_i^{\text{read}}\).

The corresponding transport-row estimate uses the same horizon and gives the short mean-transport error. Combining these two estimates on each plateau step is the history comparison used by the reader expansion. The common schedule must place the actual entrance parameter in \([t / 2, t]\) and the actual couplings in (14.63); these are the purchased plateau and coupling conditions retained in the application.

14.9.3A common chart for all comparison observables

We give the geometric check, since it prevents accidental use of different local gauges for different readers. Put \(b_x = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil\) and \(w_x = b_x^2\). At a fixed step let \(y\) be the next prescribed scale parameter, and suppose the paid room conditions hold. In explicit terms the conditions used here are

\(\displaystyle b_x \geqslant 200, \quad w \geqslant d + 10, \quad ww_y + L \leqslant Lw_x,\)

together with room nonwrapping at both levels and the prescribed reader separation of at most \(dw_x\). Here \(d\) is the fixed reader separation order. The recording parameter \(r (x)\) satisfies

\(\displaystyle x \leqslant r (x) \leqslant x^2, \qquad b_{r (x)} \leqslant 4 b_x,\)

and its flag threshold equals the healthy threshold at \(x\). Consequently \(11 b_{r (x)} + 1 \leqslant w_x\). Also the nonwrapping room bound at radius \(ww_x\) implies the local Gaussian side bound: \(100 (1 + b_{r (x)}) \leqslant 2 (ww_x + 1)\) eventually under these inequalities, and the torus side exceeds the latter quantity.

For this application fix the seed-free store: both its seed sets are empty, and its flag set is exactly the set of flags of the actual coarse field at recording threshold \(\text{fieldCut} (r (x))\). This threshold equals \(\tau_x\). Thus, for a coarse field healthy on the whole room of radius \(ww_x\), the store has no marked site in that room. Health alone would not remove seeds from an arbitrary store. For this seed-free store, the local axial construction gives one gauge on this room and a logarithmic direction of norm at most \(8 ww_x \tau_x\), where \(\tau_x\) is the healthy threshold. The purchased chart conditions place this inside half the logarithmic clearance allowance and inside the fixed chart radius. Extend that direction by zero outside the room. It remains one global direction with the same supremum bound. At any address whose distance from the room boundary is at least \(w_x\), the whole \(b_{r (x)}\)-ball lies inside the chart, and its \(11 b_{r (x)} + 1\) seed neighbourhood contains no flag. The replica bound follows from the linear replica norm bound and the exponential chart estimate. These are exactly the pose conditions used above.

It remains to fit the fine guards into that room. A fine bond in the enlarged guard room of radius \(ww_y + 8\) has parent at distance at most \(w_x + 8\) from the parent of the fine reader centre. Indeed the parent-distance inequality is

\(\displaystyle L \hspace{0.17em} \text{dist} (\text{parent} \hspace{0.17em} u, \text{parent} \hspace{0.17em} v) \leqslant \text{dist} (u, v) + L,\)

and \(ww_y + L \leqslant Lw_x\) gives the assertion. The coarse centre of a tested reader is at distance at most \(dw_x\) from the chosen root reader. Adding distances shows that every such guard bond has

\(\displaystyle \text{dist} (o, \text{parent} \hspace{0.17em} v) + w_x + 2 \leqslant (d + 2) w_x + 10 \leqslant ww_x,\)

using \(w_x \geqslant 8\) and \(w \geqslant d + 10\). Every incidence read has the same inequality with \(+ 1\) in place of \(+ 2\). Thus both endpoints of every covariance entry, and all four bonds used in every nonlinear read, lie in the one pose. Signed translations carry the reader centres, masks, forest and tag together, so the same inequalities apply to every selected orientation.

14.9.4The joint reader model on the actual sample space

Fix a forest and tag, retain this same seed-free store, and choose a chart cutoff \(\delta > 0\) with \(2 \delta\) smaller than the fixed first logarithmic chart radius of the gauge group. These are the cutoff hypotheses of the measurable field construction in the joint reader model. Use the common product space of frozen Haar links and the centered Gaussian sector coordinates from Subsection 14.15. The signed transport and the one pose gauge send the store, forest, tag and sample together. Their measure-preserving identities give one joint centered incidence vector, with covariance \(C_i^{\text{read}}\), for every reader. Equation (14.66) is therefore an entrywise comparison of that actual joint law with the short covariance, including all off-diagonal pairs.

For each reader use the guard on its enlarged fine room. The Gaussian guard estimate gives

\(\displaystyle 0 \leqslant \Gamma_a \leqslant 1, \qquad \int (1 - \Gamma_a) \hspace{0.17em} d \mu \leqslant x^{- 1} \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t)^2 .\)

On its positive set, the nonlinear comparison makes the fine field healthy on that reader patch and gives one gauge element for the whole patch. In that gauge, each normalized coordinate differs by at most \(x^{- 1 / 32} \leqslant \varepsilon_f (t)\) from the sum of the fixed parent forecast and the corresponding coordinate of the common Gaussian vector. The forecast uses the actual coarse coupling \(\beta'\); the fine field uses \(\beta\). Their conversion was included in the adjacent coupling estimate, so they are not set equal in this step.

The same construction works for every permitted reader in the fixed finite family. Its mask bound is per reader, with no union over the family, and its gauge is common to the coordinates of one patch. All readers still share the same random vector and the same parent forecast. These are precisely the quantitative receipts left explicit in the joint reader-model construction. They are obtained on the selected family whenever its purchased plateau, room, chart and coupling conditions hold. The later activity reassembly remains responsible for identifying the resulting expansion with the interacting block integral.

14.10Local Gaussian bounds in common coordinates

We combine the actual local-window comparison with the exact tree identity of Subsection 14.5. This gives both the mean and every cross-entry of the curvature covariance in the one frame used by the reader family.

Fix the patch, predictor kernel, path bounds and adjacent tower data. Their numerical costs are fixed before the finite period is chosen. Let \(x\) be the large reader parameter and \(t\) its recording parameter, chosen with

\(\displaystyle x \leqslant t \leqslant x^2, \qquad B = \lceil (\log t)^2 \rceil .\)

The recording parameter is chosen so that its coarse flag threshold equals the reader's healthy threshold. All sector matrices below use this same \(t\) and its flag set. Assume the coarse torus side is at least \(100 (1 + B)\) and retain the predictor branch and fine-side conditions of Subsection 14.5.

Use a pose of the coarse field on a room of radius \(W\) around \(o\): one gauge makes that field equal there to the exponential of one real direction \(v\), whose supremum norm is at most half the selected logarithmic clearance allowance. For every interior coarse address \(z\) with

\(\displaystyle \text{dist} (o, z) + w (x) \leqslant W,\)

assume its \(B\)-neighbourhood is in that same logarithmic chart, the replica has the prescribed clearance bound there, and no seed lies within \(11 B + 1\) of \(z\). Here \(w (x)\) is the reader's fixed room margin. These are the local conditions of the pose, not separate choices of gauges at each address.

Let \(\mathcal{U}\) be the actual active link-coordinate set for the chosen store and forest, and let \(\iota : \mathcal{U} \to V_F\) insert its coordinates into the complete forest coordinate set. Let \(H\) be the actual ordered window inverse, and let \(H^0\) be the ordered window inverse with empty store and identity coarse field, using the same forest, tag and window geometry. Write \(C = (H + H^{\textsf{T}}) / 2\) for the actual Gaussian covariance, and \(C^0 = (H^0 + (H^0)^{\textsf{T}}) / 2\) on \(V_F\) for the bare one. The prescribed mean with arbitrary frozen links is denoted \(m\); the stationary mean in the single direction \(v\) is \(m^0\).

Assume the small-window specialization in Subsection 14.7 has supplied its mean and column estimates for these actual data. These state, for every interior column \(j\) and every actual active row \(i\),

\(\displaystyle |m_j - m^0_{\iota (j)} | \leqslant t^{- 7 / 8}, \qquad |H_{ij} - H^0_{\iota (i), \iota (j)} | \leqslant t^{- 1 / 4} .\) (14.67)

Its hypotheses are precisely the chart and clearance conditions just listed. In particular its frozen-source cancellation holds for every choice of the frozen field and auxiliary channel parameters; no bound on those arbitrary frozen values is introduced here. If both \(i, j\) are interior, the two column estimates imply

\(\displaystyle |C_{ij} - C^0_{\iota (i), \iota (j)} | \leqslant t^{- 1 / 4} .\) (14.68)

This follows by adding the two inequalities and dividing by two.

14.10.1Removing the bare cut and window

Let \(A_F\) be the full flat precision on \(V_F\) identified in Subsection 14.5. The bare reference still uses a path cutoff and a principal window. We show, at the fixed patch, that

\(\displaystyle \|C^0 - A_F^{- 1} \|_0 \leqslant Ke^{- cB}\) (14.69)

for constants \(K, c > 0\) independent of the period, forest and tag. Here \(\| \cdot \|_0\) is the maximum of absolute row and column sums.

The actual path cutoff is \(r = \lfloor B / 100000 \rfloor\). Its exponentially weighted path bound gives a row tail at most \(C_1 e^{- \sigma r / 4}\) in a narrower weighted norm, with \(\sigma > 0\) fixed.
